# SF-M5: What must happen before and after an incident so that those responsible answer for it, lessons are learned, evidence survives and those affected obtain redress, including across borders?

Area M · As of clef 7924 Berichte · Luna 7924 Berichte · JEV 5667 Berichte · amos 7924 Berichte · SubQ 7924 Berichte · drex 7924 Berichte · d3-ultra 5449 Berichte · d3-flash 1674 Berichte

## Subquestions

- [SF-M5-T01](../../fragen/SF-M5/T01/): Will states require a lab, the moment a misalignment (behaviour against intended goals or values) is suspected, to preserve the state of the system, that is logs, model weights (the learned parameters) and training-data state, and to publish that it did so, before time and retraining erase the only evidence?
- [SF-M5-T02](../../fragen/SF-M5/T02/): Will each publicly reported misalignment incident that can be reproduced safely become a versioned regression test (a check that the failure does not return) that successor models must pass before release, with an independent check of the result?
- [SF-M5-T03](../../fragen/SF-M5/T03/): For every published misalignment case report, does the lab publish what changed afterwards (retraining, deployment restrictions, monitoring), and does it publish a dated follow-up showing whether those measures changed the reported behaviour on the relevant model versions and whether an outside assessor reproduced the result?
- [SF-M5-T04](../../fragen/SF-M5/T04/): Do reports that originate from a deployed model's own flagging, or from external users who observed concerning behaviour, count in the lab's disclosure process, and must each receive a reasoned answer?
- [SF-M5-T05](../../fragen/SF-M5/T05/): When a public misalignment report identifies or affects third parties, what narrow opportunity will they have to correct factual errors before publication, and who resolves a dispute without giving them a veto over publication?
- [SF-M5-T06](../../fragen/SF-M5/T06/): What duty should governments, labs and deployers have when a deployed model is later found to have violated a safety threshold: to identify, notify, correct or compensate for decisions it influenced? And how can a retrospective audit distinguish model-caused harm from the surrounding human process?
- [SF-M5-T07](../../fragen/SF-M5/T07/): Would governments accept a shared baseline for remedies when an AI system deployed in one country causes verifiable damage in another (investigation rights, evidence preservation, jurisdiction, compensation), and would the same rules also cover an incident originating in a lab in a third country?
- [SF-M5-T08](../../fragen/SF-M5/T08/): What shared minimum insurance or financial-responsibility standard would states accept for deployments of the most capable AI models that cause cross-border harm, and how would it be calibrated to price safety into the cost of scaling without becoming a compliance barrier that only the two largest labs can meet?
- [SF-M5-T09](../../fragen/SF-M5/T09/): Which economic instrument (fines, liability or procurement rules) would leave a lab's directors personally worse off for racing past an agreed safety checkpoint than for complying with it?

## Reports by share of levels 4–7

Levels 4–7 / 3–7 = probability each model gives to these levels (3 = useful background or context, 4 = contains some facts that could be cited as evidence … 7 = directly and substantially answers the question). Listed: reports where clef or Luna give levels 3–7 more than 50 %. Sorted by the higher of clef and Luna for levels 4–7. Further models (JEV, amos, d3 …) rated part of the reports; the others show “not yet rated”. The full distribution over the 8 levels is in the data file.
